# Nexus Operative continuity instructions
## v0.340.423 explicit Trial Readiness reconciliation composition

Trial Readiness can now consume one explicitly supplied, privacy-safe local
forward-trial reconciliation bundle. The bundle shares one canonical same-root,
distinct-path fence with retained history, durable orders, linked portfolio and
the owner marker. Existing non-owner files are identity/fingerprint sampled
before and after the composite review, while the owner marker stays protected by
its retained lease. The bundle's dedicated 8 MiB limit is enforced at preflight,
initial sampling and final verification.

A bundle without retained history, or one paired with a custom order gateway
that cannot expose its durable journal path, fails closed. Omitting the bundle
preserves the existing unreconciled-modeled-execution blocker. An exact bundle
can bind locally modeled BUY/SELL events, but it cannot attest source, broker,
account, funds, market realism, sustained operation, live readiness or release
acceptance. No Portal button, editable field, query parameter or automatic
private-evidence discovery was added. Engagement and optional AI remain OFF by
default; limits, account checks, reducing exits, fail-closed behavior and the
independent emergency stop remain intact. Seven beginner-friendly tabs and
black/bright-purple bottom-right hover help remain unchanged. The read-only
roadmap estimate remains exact 38% weighted (displayed about 40%, 30–45%
low-confidence), with 0/9 stages independently accepted and no release or
trading permission.


## v0.340.422 final emergency journal identity fence

The standalone local-paper emergency command now performs one final read-only
comparison against the exact public journal identity captured before its cold
replays, then rechecks the data-root identity. If the replayed journal is
replaced or becomes unobservable while the original root remains pinned, or
the root is displaced during that final journal observation,
durable confirmation is cleared and the command returns `UNCONFIRMED`. It does
not issue another halt, reset, retry or writer lock. Existing displaced-root
history remains visible but cannot produce `VERIFIED`.

A real disposable SQLite regression swaps in a separately initialized,
unhalted zero-command journal during the final latch sample and verifies the
replacement remains unmodified. This is sampled offline local emergency
evidence, not an atomic cross-process lease, native Windows/installer,
broker-paper, sustained-trial, funding, cloud, live or release acceptance.
Engagement and optional AI remain OFF by default; limits, account checks,
reducing exits, fail-closed behavior and the independent emergency stop remain
intact. No button or editable parameter was added, so seven beginner-friendly
tabs and black/bright-purple bottom-right hover help remain unchanged. The
read-only roadmap estimate remains exact 38% weighted (displayed about 40%,
30–45% low-confidence), with 0/9 stages independently accepted and no release
or trading permission.

## v0.340.421 optional Windows artifact birth time

Exact development-branch Windows run `45807905265` first failed artifact
verification and then cascaded through other artifact consumers. The run did
not retain field-level stat observations, so its exact metadata cause remains
unverified. Local reproduction identified a matching compatibility failure:
Python documents `st_birthtime_ns` as optional, while the verifier required a
compatible path/open-descriptor pair. Windows cross-source identity now keeps
device, file index, size, modification time and link count mandatory, compares
birth time only when both samples expose exact integers, and rejects malformed
exposed or unequal available values.

The initial pathname observation is retained separately and every later path
sample must preserve same-source metadata and any available birth time. Same-
source descriptor stability, regular-file and single-link checks, retained
handles, directory pinning and both complete SHA-256 passes remain strict.
Deterministic component regressions model path birth time present and
descriptor birth time absent while their cross-source ctime meanings differ,
plus pathname-only metadata changes after open. The runtime dependency floor
also moves from the advisory-
affected `cryptography` 47.x resolution to the 50.x line. Nexus uses AES-GCM
and scrypt; focused compatibility tests and a clean dependency audit are
required. This is local compatibility evidence pending an exact hosted
Windows pass, not Windows/installer acceptance, signing, provenance,
independent security clearance, broker/
account/source or sustained-trial acceptance, performance, profitability,
funding, cloud or release approval. Engagement and optional AI remain OFF by
default; limits, account checks, reducing exits, fail-closed behavior and the
independent emergency stop remain intact. No button or editable parameter was
added, so seven beginner-friendly tabs and black/bright-purple bottom-right
hover help remain unchanged. The read-only roadmap estimate remains exact 38%
weighted (displayed about 40%, 30–45% low-confidence), with 0/9 stages
independently accepted and no release or trading permission.

## v0.340.420 final direct paper BUY emergency fence

Direct paper-portfolio BUY admission now samples the independently writable
durable emergency latch both before and after exact captured-quote
revalidation. The first sample preserves emergency refusal priority when both
the cut and quote refusal are already present. The final sample is the last
fallible guard before the first accounting read or mutation, so a cut asserted
during quote validation rolls back without a position, cash delta, position
event or seal. The protected checks perform no market fetch or retry; tier-OFF
ordering remains intact and reducing SELL exits remain available.

A deterministic regression asserts the durable cut from inside the second
exact-quote validation and requires the initialization-only ledger to remain.

The direct paper-portfolio BUY path now carries its selected tier into the

## v0.340.416 tier-OFF durable BUY commit fence

A linked durable BUY fill now holds a bounded SQLite writer reservation on the
paper settings database from its authoritative tier/cap snapshot through the
order-journal and attached-portfolio commit. An OFF or cap change that commits
first is observed and refuses the BUY without a new position. A BUY that wins
the reservation commits both durable stores before OFF can acknowledge. Cold
replay verifies both order and linked-portfolio outcomes, and subsequent BUYs
remain refused after OFF.

## v0.340.415 Windows artifact stat coherence

Hosted Windows run 45807797630 exposed 92 false artifact-integrity rejections
because CPython can report path-based `stat().st_ctime_ns` as file creation
time while descriptor-based `fstat().st_ctime_ns` reports metadata change
time for the same NTFS file. Distribution verification now separates
same-source stability from path-to-handle identity. Descriptor samples still
bind device, file index, size, modification time, change time and link count.
Only the cross-source Windows comparison substitutes `st_birthtime_ns` for
the incompatible ctime field, while preserving device, file index, size,
modification time, link count, retained handles and two complete SHA-256
passes. Synthetic regressions reject changes to every retained compatibility
field.

This is offline distribution-integrity and hosted-Windows compatibility
hardening, not native installer/profile/ACL/power-loss acceptance, signing,
provenance, broker/account/source acceptance, genuine-market or sustained-trial
acceptance, performance, profitability, funding, cloud or release approval.

## v0.340.414 typed paper-POST transport fence

The disconnected single-attempt Alpaca paper boundary now requires an
OFF-by-default, one-shot typed local credential/permission lease. Opaque caller
callbacks finish before that lease and the marked request's SQLite writer-
exclusion snapshot are acquired. Cooperating local credential/permission
revocation and request-head writers therefore linearize before or after
synchronous opener entry instead of racing between ordered callbacks. The
independent emergency stop shares neither guard and is sampled last; a durable
cut observed before opener entry consumes the authorization and request as
`NOT_SENT` with zero POSTs and cannot re-arm automatically. Threaded release
regressions make these local ordering guarantees mandatory.

The final edge uses an explicitly confirmed canonical owner runtime/order path,
not a path inferred from broker evidence storage. After opaque callbacks it
rechecks both originally pinned store identities, exact reservation state and a
callback-free trusted monotonic deadline before reading that bound durable stop.

This is cooperating local linearization plus a sampled stop, not atomic or
distributed broker coordination. Provider-side credential revocation,
non-cooperating writers, other hosts and a stop asserted after the last sample

## v0.340.413 durable final-halt reserve

Durable order admission now preserves one final appendable command slot for
emergency halt whenever a state-changing command would otherwise leave the
gateway running. The invariant is enforced independently against the active-
epoch and retained-history limits before linked-ledger side effects. A reset
with two slots remaining may use the penultimate slot and a subsequent halt may
use the final slot; a reset, cancellation, expiry or other mutation cannot spend
that final slot while leaving the gateway unhalted. Duplicate no-op replays and
halted logical archival retain their existing behavior.

Read-only capacity diagnostics identify `FINAL_HALT_SLOT_RESERVED` and direct
the operator to `EMERGENCY_HALT_REQUIRED`. This is bounded local durability and
restart-safety hardening, not native storage-fault, broker/account/source,
genuine-market, sustained-trial, performance, profitability, funding, cloud,
Windows/installer or release acceptance. Engagement and optional AI remain OFF
by default; account checks, reducing exits, fail-closed behavior and the
independent emergency-stop path remain unchanged. No button or editable

## v0.340.412 detached reconciliation evidence boundary

The development risk-tier portal controls and the complete detached trial-
reconciliation stack now share one preserved two-parent history. Reconciliation
publication accepts only a closed primitive schema: exact non-negative counts,
digest fields, fill and no-fill completion flags, coherent stopped-session cut
evidence, durable halt agreement, and a recomputed event seal must all agree.
Correctly rehashed contradictory receipts, private nested values, stale seals,
invalid cut projections and non-object canonical JSON fail closed.

normalization, rejects reconciliation symlink leaves, and requires the exact
parsed bytes to match the original bounded fingerprint. Omitted bundles still
produce a blocked diagnostic for legacy modeled events without a fill digest;
supplying a bundle requires the contributing session's complete modeled history.
Execution-set hashing and bundle credit use one outcome predicate, so a
scheduler-classified BUY/SELL remains an explicit unbound incident and can
never receive excess credit.

## v0.340.410 modeled-execution reconciliation fence

Retained forward-trial review now counts every in-window modeled `BUY` or
`SELL` outcome separately and treats it as an operational incident until that
execution is explicitly bound to durable order/fill reconciliation. Raw
declared-public coverage remains visible for diagnosis, but a locally coherent
event chain and modeled-fill digest can no longer produce incident-free
coverage by themselves. HOLD-only coverage is unchanged.

The durable event schema also requires every `BUY` or `SELL` to carry its
quote-evidence digest, so it cannot be reclassified as a lifecycle record.
Matched outcomes timestamped before their bound quotes now make the retained
trace incomplete. Both cases fail closed without repairing supplied history.

Trial readiness carries the blocker through its read-only composite review
without creating or repairing an order journal. The mandatory forward release
profile requires the exact modeled-execution count, fixed blocker reason and
unchanged false authority flags. A future positive increment must bind each
execution under one coherent retained snapshot.

Windows regression fixtures now treat a distribution-directory replacement
that the kernel denies while reviewed leaf handles are pinned as a safe
rejection, while successful replacements must still surface the fixed directory
identity error. Malformed emergency-intent status tests isolate that schema
boundary from native cold-replay availability and still require one halt attempt
plus both independent replay confirmations. Production verification and
emergency-stop logic are unchanged; exact-child hosted Windows evidence remains
required.

This is bounded fail-closed local evidence classification, not positive

The Windows installer smoke no longer performs an unbounded wait for redirected
stdout/stderr after the root process exits. Both drain tasks share one fixed
10-second grace; failure records a failed phase, closes owned readers and
prevents installer-result evidence from being written. Evidence reports the
maximum 300-second root-process wait and 10-second output-drain budgets as
separate fields rather than presenting either as a whole-phase wall clock.

can raise a sharing violation while the already validated descriptor is being

boundary instead of treating a post-validation race as an initial input error.

## Active M1-M9 execution discipline

Before every development edit, recover the fresh development head, active stacked reviews,
exact-head CI and current blockers, then read the read-only
`snapshot()['milestone_execution_plan']` and `snapshot()['milestone_work_batches']` together
with the D1-D9 acceptance stages. Select the largest coherent safe batch from the highest
priority milestone that does not overlap an active write. External broker, genuine-feed,
native-Windows or independent-review blockers do not justify stalling: continue separate
offline candidates while preserving the blocker and required evidence exactly. Parallelize
independent read-only inspection and tests, but serialize repository writes and publish only
with an expected-head lease.

Every M1-M9 task remains `REVIEW_REQUIRED` until its stated evidence is independently
available; code, version numbers, test counts, synthetic scores and local hashes are not task
acceptance. Do not raise the weighted 38% baseline merely for implementing or testing a task.
Keep release approval, trading permission and launch-date prediction separate, preserve OFF
defaults, limits, account checks, fail-closed exits, independent emergency stop, seven beginner
tabs and black/bright-purple bottom-right hover help, and never publish private broker/account
evidence or credentials.

## v0.340.321 provider transaction acknowledgement binding

Provider POST and GET write contexts now retain their captured SQLite main-file identity through transaction finalization, require the exact main connection and file before close, and sample the same pathname identity again after close. Existing and new contexts both reject an uncommitted return. A during-transaction replacement or injected post-commit identity fault fails closed, preserves the replacement, and leaves any committed pending reservation unavailable for automatic retry or capacity reuse; exception unwinding also rechecks an existing main path. Hosted Windows regression exposed that the disposable provider-result fixture compared a long frozen journal path with the temporary root's 8.3 spelling; it now derives each private relative manifest name against either the caller's lexical root or that exact root's resolved spelling, rejecting paths outside both, with an alias-path regression. The release-trial wrapper also preserves only reviewed fixed paper-tool failure codes without exposing child output, paths or private inputs. This is a sampled local acknowledgement boundary and evidence-path correction, not an atomic directory lease against a non-cooperating change after the final sample, native Windows durability, provider-wide coordination, broker/account/source acceptance, routing, funds, live or release authority. Runtime OFF defaults, limits, account checks, fail-closed exits and emergency-stop independence are unchanged. No UI controls changed: seven beginner tabs and black/bright-purple bottom-right hover help remain intact. Broker-paper, genuine-feed 72-hour trials, native ordinary-user Windows acceptance, funding/live/cloud and owner release approval remain outstanding. Implementation remains weighted 38% (30–45%, low confidence) and 0/9 stages independently accepted.

## v0.340.317 exact Windows emergency-parent lease

The native emergency-latch lease now opens the exact public Windows parent path with `OPEN_REPARSE_POINT` instead of following a pre-resolved path, and validates the resulting handle as a directory without reparse attributes before any latch entry access. The Windows regression contract also now recognizes ordinary access denial when the still-open latch file itself blocks a parent rename; that is conservative prevention, not evidence that the sampled-identity fence failed. ## v0.340.300 paper pacing main-connection binding

The paper-read pacing writer preflighted its public SQLite path before opening it, but it did not require the resulting connection to report that exact path as its only `main` database or compare the connection's existing serialized main content with the exact bounded public main file before application mutation. A pathname/connection substitution or faulty connection seam could therefore direct a valid-looking append at a different or changed store.

v0.340.300 pins the pre-resolved single-link main-file identity across connection and setup boundaries, requires one exact SQLite `main` binding, and compares bounded serialized connection bytes with a no-follow fingerprint of the public main file before any Nexus append. A retained rollback journal is recovered under the same lease; after `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` performs SQLite's recovery, the recovered connection and exact main-file bytes must agree before application mutation. Binding/content disagreements fail closed with fixed codes, and raw path errors at the size boundary are contained. Existing DELETE mode, size bounds, account/schema binding, hash-chain replay, ownership fencing, cooldown, pending-review and recovery requirements remain mandatory.

## v0.340.48 strict stable distribution manifest

Distribution manifest creation and verification now reject non-regular, symlinked, hardlinked, empty, oversized or read-mutated artifacts and require stable descriptor metadata while hashing. ## v0.340.25 request-journal writer exclusion during POST recovery

The explicit resolved-unknown recovery path acquires shared POST ownership first, then a write-excluding exact-head request-journal snapshot. Both terminal proof reads replay the held journal and verify its pathname still names the opened file. The POST-budget recovery transaction remains nested inside those guards, so a competing request writer cannot land between the final proof and budget commit; a same-content pathname replacement is detected before commit and rolls the budget append back. Direct tests exercise both races and require unchanged request or budget bytes.

## v0.340.24 cancelled-lifespan failure observation

If ASGI lifespan cancellation arrives while the private shutdown worker is running, the application now waits synchronously for that exact worker, retrieves its terminal result and cancels the pending asyncio wrapper before returning. Successful cleanup re-raises cancellation; cleanup failure overrides cancellation so shutdown cannot appear clean or leave an orphaned future exception. Direct tests cover both outcomes and require the private lifecycle thread to be gone with no event-loop exception report.

A cursor or budget reader that rewrites either journal and raises cannot hide the mutation behind generic recovery uncertainty. Eight direct regressions cover the two reader families, both read phases and both journal targets. The injected fixture rewrites the settled budget and raises from the post-result cursor read; the release validator and negative control make the fixed file-set refusal mandatory.

## v0.340.15 pre-commit reconciliation budget fingerprint

Explicit checkpoint reconciliation already pinned the two journal identities and cursor content from entry, but it captured reconnect-budget content only after the terminal result stage. ## v0.340.10 fresh-reservation checkpoint ownership

The reconnect coordinator previously required `duplicate_checkpoint` to be a boolean but did not require it to be false after granting this caller the sole fresh provider request. A corrupted or overlapping acknowledgement could therefore claim both that the cursor was written and that the checkpoint was a replay, then close the reconnect budget automatically. A fresh reservation now rejects duplicate checkpoint acknowledgements, retains the exact v6 checkpoint plus pending budget, and requires the established explicit no-network reconciliation path. This performs no second provider request. The v0.340.9 Windows portability and complete-history audit fixes are retained.

Final local validation passed 4,386 tests with one expected non-Windows skip and one existing Starlette/httpx warning; the reconnect-budget suite passed 325, the direct cursor/checkpoint/reconnect set passed 426, the complete Activity SSE set passed 524 and the merged Windows/audit/reconnect/release set passed 678. ## v0.340.9 Windows portability and complete-history audit repair

Exact v0.340.8 source ced0884bb08acad99654042ea8a2c7f24a2678b9 failed security 40735154460, safety 40735154501 and Windows 40735154550. Packaging was skipped, so no current verified installer follows from these runs.

The backup now explicitly closes its target SQLite connection before any failure cleanup. Retained snapshot inspection uses a fresh no-follow path stat rather than Windows DirEntry metadata, whose link-count fields can be zero. The shared file fingerprint compares stable identity/size/mtime/link fields across path and handle APIs, checks creation-time stability separately inside each API, and retains path-API creation time plus the content digest for later comparisons. POSIX cross-API creation-time equality remains required. Identity, single-link, sidecar, byte-cap and changed-file refusals remain intact.

The actual reachable-history scan reproduced HISTORY_BYTE_CAP: 9,422 objects total 548,857,439 uncompressed bytes, just beyond 512 MiB. The finite default is now 1 GiB, with no ref/object omission, pruning or private-report exceptions. An incomplete audit exposes only an allowlisted reason code. The same 813-commit/5,867-blob reachable history passed the expanded scan with zero findings; current-candidate artifact and fresh exact-source security/Gitleaks checks are separate requirements.

Preserve all existing safety fences and require fresh exact-source CI; owner-profile, broker/market, funds/live/cloud and independent/owner acceptance remain open.

## v0.340.8 transport-bound recovery closure

The primary checkpoint acknowledgement path required a transport-bound v6 cursor record, but its lost/malformed-acknowledgement recovery helper and the explicit no-network reconciliation path still accepted a reservation-bound v4 record. ## v0.340.6 durable checkpoint review-count binding

The complete cursor profile already journaled the review input digest and all four aggregate counts, but the reconnect acknowledgement validator checked only that returned counts were bounded and arithmetically consistent. A coherently altered replay/deduplication pair could therefore remain plausible without matching the committed checkpoint. The durable public binding now carries the journaled digest and bounded counts, and the coordinator requires exact equality before closing the reservation. A mismatch returns fixed checkpoint-state uncertainty after the original single provider request and preserves the split state for explicit no-network reconciliation.

## v0.340.5 canonical checkpoint acknowledgement boundary

The reconnect coordinator previously validated the acknowledgement fields it consumed but later copied the entire checkpoint return into its public result. An unexpected internal extension could therefore cross the boundary even though it was neither canonical nor durable evidence. The coordinator now requires an exact built-in mapping with the complete key set, fixed checkpoint policy and limitations, digest-only identity fields and bounded review-count relationships before validating the durable cursor binding. Noncanonical returns preserve the exact split state for explicit no-network reconciliation and return fixed checkpoint-state uncertainty after the original single provider request.

## v0.340.4 checkpoint transport-provenance binding

The reconnect coordinator previously required internally consistent provider-access, stream-connection, reconnect and source fields, but did not compare them with whether the call actually used the injected opener seam. A corrupted checkpoint return could flip all four fields coherently and falsely report actual provider HTTPS access while still matching the durable cursor record. The coordinator now derives the expected provenance from its own call boundary and rejects any disagreement before closing the reconnect result.

## v0.340.3 durable reservation-freshness binding

The coordinator previously proved that a returned reservation matched the exact pending journal record but trusted the returned `duplicate_reservation` and `provider_request_authorized` booleans. A corrupted replay acknowledgement could therefore relabel an existing reservation as fresh and perform another provider read. The coordinator now captures a totally validated pre-BEGIN budget view and requires both acknowledgement booleans to agree with whether that reservation already existed. A concurrent reservation appearing between the snapshot and BEGIN is rejected rather than promoted.

## v0.340.2 authoritative result-denial preservation

The shared result-write recovery wrapper previously treated every exception from `finish` as a possibly lost acknowledgement. If the exact result had already committed, a current typed store-binding, clock, head, confirmation or other state denial could therefore be replaced by the older terminal result's exact cold replay. The wrapper now permits typed recovery only for `ALPACA_ACTIVITY_SSE_RECONNECT_BUDGET_RESULT_ACKNOWLEDGEMENT_UNCERTAIN`. Generic transport loss and malformed returned acknowledgements still recover only through complete validated replay. Four integration regressions cover successful checkpointing, failed-provider scheduling, trusted-time uncertainty retirement and explicit no-network checkpoint reconciliation.

## v0.340.1 production fixed-diagnostic recovery match

The safety-closure recovery wrapper previously compared a typed error with an unprefixed code even though the production `_fail` boundary always emits the fixed `ALPACA_ACTIVITY_SSE_RECONNECT_BUDGET_` prefix. A closure whose terminal write committed and then raised its own typed acknowledgement-uncertainty diagnostic could therefore bypass the exact recovery attempted for a generic lost channel. The wrapper now matches only that complete fixed diagnostic before cold-replaying the exact requested terminal record. Other typed denials are still re-raised without recovery, so an older terminal state cannot override current confirmation, head, clock, stop or state failures.

## v0.340.0 no-network safety-closure acknowledgement recovery

No-network reconnect safety closures now treat a structurally returned acknowledgement as transport rather than durable evidence. After the outcome-clock uncertainty close, pending emergency-stop abort or retry-wait emergency-stop close returns, the wrapper ignores the tuple and cold-replays the private reconnect-budget journal. Only the exact requested terminal record may continue; an uncommitted forged return, a wrong terminal record or a same-inode rewrite remains fail-closed. These functions have no credential, cursor or provider boundary and grant no retry authority.

## Historical continuity boundary

This file intentionally retains the current v0.340 continuity window rather than duplicating the repository's complete release history. Older checkpoint prose remains recoverable from Git, versioned `validation/release_trials_*.json` evidence, `ROADMAP.md`, `VERSIONING.md`, and `LAUNCH_DRIVE.md`. Do not infer acceptance from an omitted historical paragraph.

For every future sprint, recover the latest remote development head and current CI first; use isolated work and an expected-head lease; keep engagement permissions OFF by default; preserve bounded limits, account checks, fail-closed exits, the independent emergency stop, beginner tabs, and black/bright-purple bottom-right hover help. Never publish private credentials, account data, or retained market evidence. No broker, funds, live, cloud, release, or owner authority follows from synthetic tests, local hashes, version numbers, or this compacted continuity record.
